      - filings
      - interim-operation
      - transition
      reason: The failed research run produced no verified authority that changes
        the saved working position.
      answer_effect: Continue to treat state licensing as a day-one launch condition
        and do not count Harbor-only states in Mosaic's operating footprint without
        confirmed authority.
      references: []
    alternative_paths:
    - title: State-based migration hold
      proposed_change: Close the acquisition but block regulated activity for customers
        in states where Mosaic lacks confirmed authority.
      benefit: Separates the corporate closing from the regulated service launch.
      tradeoff: Creates a delayed or uneven customer migration.
      remaining_condition: The product must reliably identify and restrict affected
        customers.
      question_keys:
      - transferability
      - filings
    - title: Harbor transition or run-off
      proposed_change: Keep Harbor as the legal provider for a limited period while
        Mosaic obtains authority.
      benefit: May preserve service where a regulator permits the structure.
      tradeoff: Requires Harbor to remain operational, licensed, funded, bonded, and
        responsible after close.
      remaining_condition: The structure must be permitted in each relevant state
        and match the actual contracts, control, and funds flow.
      question_keys:
      - interim-operation
      - transition
    integrated_answer: Harbor's licenses should not be treated as assets that automatically
      transfer to Mosaic. Mosaic should launch in a state only under its own confirmed
      authority or a verified regulator-approved transition. The four Harbor-only
      states are clear launch gates, while overlapping states still require transaction
      and transition review. If approval cannot be completed before close, block regulated
      activity in the affected states or use a genuine Harbor run-off structure where
      permitted.
    next_step: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete a state-by-state
      matrix covering license status, transaction notice, pre-close approval, interim
      authority, outstanding obligations, and surrender conditions.

        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
        text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL regimes
          and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue

        text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states (per
          the request).
        reason: Needed to continue

        text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which affects
          whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer (structure
          not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue

      questions:
      - question_id: Q-SAR
        text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
          been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
        consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they can
          create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and could affect
          the deal itself.
        state: answered
        answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
          in diligence.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-070aec
      - question_id: Q-KYC
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
          is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
          and recordkeeping obligations?
        consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
          gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping obligations
          for these users.
        state: answered
        answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
          reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-620c62
      - question_id: Q-MTL
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
          is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
          those customers?
        consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends on
          license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
          question.
        state: answered
        answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses for
          the four new markets.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-771ee1
      - question_id: Q-BONUS
        text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
        consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
          disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
        state: left_open
        answer: null
        answer_kind: null
        linked_fact_ids: []
      decisions: []
      files: {}

      problem_analysis:

        analysis:
          schema_version: 1
          objective: Complete the Harbor asset acquisition without causing Mosaic
            Relay to conduct money transmission without valid state authority.
          proposed_method: Move Harbor users and balances into Mosaic Relay on day
            one and use or assume Harbor's money transmitter licenses in states where
            Mosaic lacks a license.
          framing_note: The key question is not only whether a license can be assigned
            by contract. The analysis must identify the licensed entity, the post-close
            provider, state filing and approval requirements, authority during any
            interim period, and the treatment of outstanding obligations. No external
            authority was retrieved, so state-specific conclusions remain unresolved.
          parts:
          - key: asset-sale
            label: Asset acquisition
            description: Mosaic Relay is buying Harbor Pay's assets rather than acquiring
              Harbor's equity.
            category: relationship
            status: reported
            references:

              record_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06

            category: activity
            status: reported
            references:
            - kind: fact
              record_id: FACT-20260909-551367

              in 41, with four states attributed only to Harbor's footprint.

            label: Planned reliance on Harbor licenses

              for the four new markets.
            category: constraint
            status: reported
            references:
            - kind: fact
              record_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
          - key: post-close-operator

            description: The migration design suggests Mosaic will become the service

              of obligations have not been supplied.
            category: actor

            question: Can any Harbor license transfer to Mosaic or remain effective
              for Mosaic's activity after the asset sale?
            why_it_matters: Without valid authority, Mosaic may be unable to offer
              money transmission in the affected state.
            kind: applicability

            answer_effect: Continue to treat state licensing as a day-one launch condition
              and do not count Harbor-only states in Mosaic's operating footprint
              without confirmed authority.
            references: []
          alternative_paths:
          - title: State-based migration hold
            proposed_change: Close the acquisition but block regulated activity for
              customers in states where Mosaic lacks confirmed authority.
            benefit: Separates the corporate closing from the regulated service launch.
            tradeoff: Creates a delayed or uneven customer migration.
            remaining_condition: The product must reliably identify and restrict affected
              customers.
            question_keys:
            - transferability
            - filings
          - title: Harbor transition or run-off
            proposed_change: Keep Harbor as the legal provider for a limited period
              while Mosaic obtains authority.
            benefit: May preserve service where a regulator permits the structure.
            tradeoff: Requires Harbor to remain operational, licensed, funded, bonded,
              and responsible after close.
            remaining_condition: The structure must be permitted in each relevant
              state and match the actual contracts, control, and funds flow.
            question_keys:
            - interim-operation
            - transition
          integrated_answer: Harbor's licenses should not be treated as assets that
            automatically transfer to Mosaic. Mosaic should launch in a state only
            under its own confirmed authority or a verified regulator-approved transition.
            The four Harbor-only states are clear launch gates, while overlapping
            states still require transaction and transition review. If approval cannot
            be completed before close, block regulated activity in the affected states
            or use a genuine Harbor run-off structure where permitted.
          next_step: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete a state-by-state
            matrix covering license status, transaction notice, pre-close approval,
            interim authority, outstanding obligations, and surrender conditions.

        objective: Complete the Harbor asset acquisition without causing Mosaic Relay
          to conduct money transmission without valid state authority.
        proposed_method: Move Harbor users and balances into Mosaic Relay on day one
          and use or assume Harbor's money transmitter licenses in states where Mosaic
          lacks a license.
        framing_note: The key question is not only whether a license can be assigned
          by contract. The analysis must identify the licensed entity, the post-close
          provider, state filing and approval requirements, authority during any interim
          period, and the treatment of outstanding obligations. No external authority
          was retrieved, so state-specific conclusions remain unresolved.
